Gold outlook:  Gold has remained resilient despite a more 
hawkish Fed and higher real yields, suggesting much of the 
tightening is already reflected in prices. With markets 
discounting an aggressive Fed path, we see an increasingly 
asymmetric risk -reward: limited downsid e risk from further 
rate repricing, but meaningful upside should Fed rate 
expectations shift towards being less restrictive. Official -
sector demand has slowed from recent years but has become 
broader based, while investor positioning remains well below 
levels seen at the start of the 2022 hiking cycle. 
Oil outlook: Oil prices have risen sharply as recent attacks 
cast greater uncertainty over Saudi export s, even as Gulf 
crude flows overall remain resilient. Other regional producers 
have helped sustain exports, containing the immediate supply 
impact. However, adequate US commercial crude stocks 
mask thinner buffers elsewhere, with strategic reserves and 
fuel inventories low. This leaves the m arket more vulnerable 
to a prolonged disruption, supporting a higher geopolitical risk 
premium and an upward revision to our outlook.

15 Gold, crude oil – at a glance

Anthony Naab, CFA 
Investment Strategist

### Page 16

Global Market Outlook 16

USD view

Further Fed tightening supports the USD near term. We expect the USD to remain supported, with our 3-month DXY forecast 
at 100.2. Resilient US payrolls for August alongside sticky energy and IT price -driven inflation reinforce the Fed's focus on 
containing inflation. Following its 25bp hike to 3.75–4.00%, we expect another hike by end-2026 and potentially another one in 
early H1 2027. Further tightening should keep US front -end yields elevated and support the USD. However, money markets 
already price a more aggressive Fed hiking path than our expectations, limiting the scope for further USD gains and leaving the 
USD vulnerable if incoming data weaken the case for additional hikes. 
Fading inflation and narrowing rate advantage point to a softer USD in the medium term. We expect the USD index (DXY) 
to decline gradually towards 98 over 12 months. Inflation pressures are expected to moderate  as the impact of oil prices and 
tariffs fade, reducing the need for continued Fed tightening. We forecast that the Fed funds rate may peak in H1 2027  in the 
current mini-rate hike cycle and then gradually decline thereafter as inflation moves closer towards target , supporting our DXY 
call. Further ECB and BoJ tightening should also progressively narrow the USD's relative rate advantage, supporting gradual 
USD moderation. Resilient US growth and elevated government bond yields should, however, limit the pace of depreciation.

Near-term EUR weakness; constructive medium term. The ECB raised its deposit 
rate 25bps to 2.50% in September. Potential ECB tightening should narrow the US-
Euro area rate differential over time, supporting EUR over 12 months.

GBP/USD 1.3219 1.33 1.35 
Rangebound with near term downside risk: UK inflation rose to 3.1% y/y, keeping 
BoE tightening risk alive, but softer labour conditions should limit the extent of 
tightening. Fed -BoE policy divergence may constrain GBP near term, while an 
expected peak in US rates by mid -2027 should allow a modes t recovery over the 
medium term. The Autum Budget in October could increase volatility.

USD/JPY 158.86 157 153 
Constructive JPY. The BoJ's September 25bp hike to 1.25% reinforces policy 
normalisation. The still wide  US-Japan yield differential may limit near-term JPY 
gains, but further BoJ tightening should gradually narrow the gap and support JPY 
over the medium term towards our target of 153.

AUD/USD 0.7012 0.73 0.74 
Near-term AUD weakness; constructive medium term: Australia’s Q2 GDP grew 
0.4% q/q, reinforcing domestic resilience. A relatively restrictive RBA stance supports 
AUD, while eventual narrowing in relative rate differentials should improve the 
medium-term outlook.

NZD/USD 0.5662 0.56 0.57 
Rangebound: The RBNZ raised rates 25bps to 2.75%, while New Zealand’s Q2 
GDP grew 0.2% q/q. Improving growth provides support, but an uneven recovery and 
near-term USD strength should limit NZD gains.

USD/CAD 1.4138 1.39 1.37 
Near-term CAD weakness; modest medium-term recovery: Canada employment 
fell 41,700 in August vs. a 15,000 expected rise, while the BoC held rates at 2.25%. 
Softer labour conditions and Fed-BoC divergence may constrain CAD near term.

USD/CHF 0.8278 0.82 0.79 
Near-term CHF weakness; medium-term CHF recovery: Swiss inflation surprised 
higher at 0.8% y/y, but the wide Fed -SNB rate differential should continue to favour 
USD near term. As Fed tightening peaks and the US rate advantage narrows, CHF 
should gradually recover over 12 months.

USD/CNH 6.7159 6.70 6.67 
Gradual CNH appreciation: China’s industrial output in August rose 5.2% y/y, 
beating estimates, but retail sales growth slowed to 0.4% on weak domestic demand. 
Production resilience and PBoC currency support favour modest CNH appreciation, 
while soft consumption and an easing policy bias should limit the pace of gains.

USD/SGD 1.2794 1.28 1.26 
Rangebound near term; constructive SGD medium term: Further Fed tightening 
and elevated US yields may limit SGD appreciation near term. Resilient 
fundamentals and MAS's exchange -rate policy should support gradual SGD 
appreciation as US rate support fades.

USD/INR 95.96 – 98 
Modestly bearish INR: India’s crude oil imports rose 26% y/y in August, and a USD 
77bn AI import deficit are both adding to structural import pressures. Higher -for-
longer US rates remain a headwind, while capital inflows and RBI intervention should 
smooth out near-term volatility and support a gradual INR depreciation towards 98.

USD/MYR 4.0865 – 4.00 Modestly bullish MYR: Resilient domestic demand, strong exports and corporate FX conversion support MYR; external uncertainty should keep appreciation gradual.

USD/KRW 1,368.9 – 1,540 
Bearish KRW: Recent KRW -supportive corporate USD selling is fading, while 
reduced National Pension Service’s FX hedging and prospect of USD 350bn US 
investment package by the South Korean national investment committee could 
increase USD demand. Higher US rates add a he adwind, supporting KRW 
depreciation over 12 months.

### Page 18

Global Market Outlook 18

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) reduced its Overweight allocation to global equities to +1 from +3 (maximum +5) 
in September. The downgrade was driven primarily by a weaker valuation signal as valuation score fell to -2 for both Developed 
Market and Asian equities. Despite some moderation, fundamentals remain strongly supportive of equities. Positive factors 
include resilient net earnings upgrades, a positive economic surprise index, and PMI new orders remaining above 50, signalling 
continued expansion in industrial activity. Market technicals weakened but continued to support equities overall. Our market 
breadth indicator shows that 72% of equity markets remain above their 200 -day moving averages. Stock net advances are 
positive and not at levels that would typically signal an imminent market reversal. The stock-bond model continues to outperform 
strongly, generating 4.2% alpha YTD relative to the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark. 
Our 1–3 month equity market regime models continue to signal low bear market risk, with probabilities of just 0.7% for 
S&P 500 and 5.0% for MSCI AC World  indices. The low readings continued to be driven by subdued option -market implied 
volatility. At 14 – 15%, the VIX and market cap -weighted one-month implied volatility measures for Europe and US remain 
comfortably below the 20% threshold at which bear market risk tends to rise sharply. While momentum indicators have softened, 
they point to a typical market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful market stress.  
Our positioning indicators are not signalling any reversal risk. Previous long positioning in DXY has fully unwound and our 
market diversity indicator for MSCI Singapore has also normalized after the index corrected from its September peak.

15 Quant perspective: Reducing Equity 
Overweight as Valuation Weakened

Francis Lim Senior Quantitative Strategist Maggie, Au Yeung Quantitative Analyst

### Page 19

Global Market Outlook 19 
Fig. 32 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Reduce equity Overweight 20% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +2. 3-month change in PMI new 
orders indicate a slower pace in the expansion of 
industrial activity. All other factors such as net 
earnings upgrades, economic surprise index and 
risk sentiment remain positive.  
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded 1 
standard deviation of historical average.  
• Technical: +1. Net-advances in global stocks are 
positive but not extreme. Market breadth declined 
as equity markets above their 200-day moving 
averages fell from 85% to 72%, but it remains 
above the model’s 60% threshold. 
Estimated probability hovers around 20 – 21% 
• Conference Board consumer confidence 
remains weak at -8.6% y/y. 
• PMI new orders-to-inventory ratio is at 1.1x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x.  
• Conference Board leading indicators 
contracted by -1.1% y/y.  
• Fed funds rate-to-natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.6x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x. 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors. 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate. 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa. 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above.

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World indices None flagged 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. The 
probabilities of bear market sit at just 0.7% and 
5.0%, respectively, driven by subdued options-
market implied volatility. Momentum across both 
indices have weakened but they point to a typical 
market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful  
market stress.  
• Previous long positioning in the USD index 
(DXY) has fully unwound after DXY fell 
steeply form its peak in late July. The renewed 
strength in DXY since the Fed’s 16 Sept rate 
hike has started from neutral positioning.  
• Market diversity indicator for MSCI 
Singapore has normalised after the index 
corrected from its September peak. 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc. 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time.  
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses Machine Learning to forecast market regimes 
or future trends based on identified market drivers. 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions.

Source: Standard Chartered

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid -life wealth planning or fortifying assets  for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Purpose 
Today,  
Tomorrow,  
Forever 
Our approach to helping you grow and manage 
your wealth starts with you. We use a goals -
aware approach to understand your vision of 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever for yourself, your 
family and beyond, and then design portfolios to 
meet your various needs.  
Using our ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ 
approach, we ensure your wealth needs for the 
near term (Today) are met, while ensuring your 
wealth needs for the decades ahead (Tomorrow 
and Forever) are also planned for.  
Your vision of ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ is 
unique to you. Our specialists partner with you to 
build well -diversified, long -term Foundation 
portfolios, aligned to your Today, Tomorrow and 
Forever needs. Opportunistic ideas are added to 
capture short -term opportunities, a nd sufficient 
protection is included to address the objectives of 
you and your family.

flows take centre stage

### Page 25

Global Market Outlook 25

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi -asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer -term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

approach to

However, markets constantly evolve and your 
needs change. Hence, we encourage you to 
undertake regular portfolio reviews to ensure your 
portfolio remains aligned to your Today, 
Tomorrow and Forever objectives. This proactive 
approach includes strategic reb alancing based 
on insights from our Chief Investment Office.

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 19 - 23
Date:    1 hour ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 19 - 23

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 19 - 23
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week’s inflation reports came in hot. September’s CPI rose 0.6%
m/m, lifting headline inflation from 3.4% y/y in August to 3.7%. Core CPI
rose 0.3% m/m, taking its y/y rate from 2.4% to 2.6%. Both topped
expectations. PPI final demand jumped 1.3% m/m. That pushed its y/y
rate to 6.3%, the highest since 2022. Retail sales rose 0.8% m/m, though
just 0.2% excluding gas stations.
Odds of a 25bps hike at the October 27-28 FOMC meeting fell from 22%

before the CPI report to 17%, despite the hot inflation data. Fed Chair
Kevin Warsh offered no signal on October's meeting at the IMF and World
Bank annual meetings in Bangkok on Friday. “I don’t prejudge meetings,”
he said. Odds of a December hike rose. We still expect the Fed to reverse
the remaining two of last year’s three 25bps “insurance” cuts. Fed officials
are now in their pre-meeting blackout period.
This week’s data calendar is light, with earnings season taking center

stage. Friday’s flash PMIs are the main US releases. The Fed’s Financial
Stability Report and Canada’s federal budget are tentatively due Tuesday.
Here’s more:
(1) ADP & Unemployment Claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading
(Tue) follows a rise in the four-week average to 24,750 for the week ended
September 26, the highest since early June and up from a late-July
bottom of 8,250 (chart).
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 199,000, with the four-week

average at 198,500 (chart). Continuing claims fell to 1,710,000 for the

### Page 4

week ended October 2, matching their four-week average. The labor
market continues to improve.
(2) Flash PMIs. S&P Global’s flash M-PMI and NM-PMI for October (Fri)
follow September readings of 55.9 and 58.8 (chart). The services prices
component is worth watching after ISM’s NM-PMI prices-paid index
jumped to 70.7 in September, the highest since July 2022.
Flash PMIs for Australia and Japan (Thu) and for the Eurozone, Germany,
France, and the UK (Fri) also land this week. September’s M-PMIs were

52.9 for the Eurozone and 52.1 for the UK.
(3) Earnings. The earnings reporting season picks up this week. Netflix,
GE Aerospace, and Northrop Grumman report on Tuesday. Tesla and
Meta, the first of the Magnificent-7 to report, follow on Wednesday along

### Page 5

with Lam Research, IBM, and Texas Instruments. Spotify, Bloom Energy,
Western Digital, Lockheed Martin, and Blackstone report on Thursday.
ASML and TSMC posted record Q3 results last week. ASML’s net sales
rose to €11.7 billion from €9.3 billion in Q2, with the gross margin up to
57.5% from 54.0%. TSMC’s revenue jumped 50.9% y/y. Analysts’
consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for S&P 500
companies of 24.0% y/y (chart).
(4) Global. China’s Q3 GDP (Mon) follows Q2’s 4.3% y/y growth (chart).

We expect a moderate pickup in activity. China’s unemployment rate for
September is also due, following August’s 5.2% rate.
UK CPI (Wed) for September follows August’s 3.1% y/y headline and
2.6% core (chart). Rising energy and input costs point to a further pickup,

### Page 6

with the input PPI up 8.2% y/y in Q2. Bank of England Governor Andrew
Bailey speaks later that day. UK labor market data (Tue) and PPI (Wed)
are also due.
Canada’s CPI (Mon) for September follows August’s 3.0% y/y headline
and 2.3% core (chart).

Document 323: Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO?
Date:    6 hours ago
Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Irrational Exuberance In S&P
500 FEMO?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 21 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Chris Geirman / Unsplash
Are analysts' earnings expectations for the S&P 500 irrationally
exuberant? So far this year, FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum, as
measured by actual earnings per share) has been soaring, while FOMO
(fear of missing out, as reflected in P/E multiples) has been mostly
retreating. Much of that earnings optimism is concentrated in the S&P 500
Information Technology sector, particularly the Semiconductors industry,

### Page 3

where the AI investment boom has fueled extraordinary demand, pricing
power, and profit margins. But can these conditions persist? Let's take a
closer look:
(1) FEMO overwhelming FOMO. The S&P 500 is up 13.3% ytd, despite a
13.5% contraction in its forward P/E. That's because FEMO has
overwhelmed waning FOMO, with forward earnings jumping 31.0% since
the start of the year (chart).
The divergence is even more striking in Information Technology. The

sector's forward earnings has soared 65.5% ytd, while its forward P/E has
plunged 25.9%. The S&P 500 IT stock price index has risen 22.6% ytd,
powered by FEMO (chart).

### Page 4

Industry analysts remain remarkably bullish on the S&P 500's
fundamentals. Forward revenues is up 15.7% y/y, while forward earnings
is up 39.0% (chart).
(2) Semiconductors driving FEMO. Much of that optimism originates in
the IT sector, where forward revenues and earnings are up 57.2% and
91.4% y/y, respectively (chart).
Digging deeper reveals that the sector's extraordinary FEMO originates
largely in the Semiconductors industry, where forward revenues and

earnings are up 121.7% and 176.0% y/y, respectively (chart).

### Page 5

Indeed, semiconductors now account for a record 55.0% of the IT sector's
forward earnings and 40.9% of its market capitalization (chart).
The growing importance of semiconductors extends to the broader
market. Semiconductors now account for a record 20.4% of the S&P 500’s
forward earnings and 15.8% of its market capitalization (chart).

### Page 6

That makes the industry's earnings trajectory increasingly important for
the entire index. Analysts expect Semiconductor revenues and earnings to
grow 61.3% and 74.0% over the next year, respectively. More remarkably,
they expect earnings growth to average 66.1% annually over the next five
years.
These extraordinary expectations help explain why analysts project IT
sector earnings growth of 44.8% over the next year and 42.0% annually
over the next five years (chart).

In turn, analysts expect S&P 500 earnings to grow 19.0% over the next
year and an average of 26.1% annually over the next five years, the latter
approaching a record high.

### Page 7

(3) Pricing power fueling Semiconductors' FEMO. To determine
whether S&P 500 FEMO shows irrational exuberance, we need to assess
whether Semiconductor FEMO does so. Much of the industry's
extraordinary earnings growth reflects unprecedented profit margin
expansion. The industry’s forward profit margin nearly doubled from the
2023 lows to a record 51.8% during the week of October 20 (chart).
The explanation is straightforward: Demand for chips has surged as

hyperscalers and neocloud providers have raced to build AI infrastructure,
while supply is capacity constrained. The resulting pricing pressures are
clearly evident in the PPI for electronic components & accessories (up
26.8% y/y through September) and the import price index for computer &
electronic product manufacturing (up 12.4% y/y through September)
(chart).

### Page 8

(4) Will Semiconductors' pricing power persist? That's the critical
question for assessing whether Semiconductor FEMO is irrationally
exuberant. Investors already seem skeptical. The Semiconductor
industry's forward P/E has plunged to just 14.2 despite surging earnings
expectations (chart). In other words, unlike the tech bubble of 1999, any
irrational exuberance in FEMO has been mostly offset by depressed
FOMO!
The goods news is that demand for chips should remain strong. The nine

largest cloud service providers are expected to spend $957 billion on
capex this year, up 106% y/y, and more than $1.5 trillion in 2027. But
supply is starting to catch up. Global semiconductor manufacturing
capacity is expected to expand 5% this year and another 6% in 2027,
potentially easing shortages and price pressures.
(5) Irrational earnings exuberance? We suspect there is some irrational
exuberance in Semiconductor FEMO, particularly in analysts' consensus

long-term earnings growth expectations. Today's demand-supply
imbalance supports extraordinary profits, but sustaining current pricing
power and margins over a longer time horizon seems optimistic as
capacity expands. With Semiconductors accounting for more than one-
fifth of S&P 500 forward earnings, that exuberance is reflected in the
broader index.
Nevertheless, FEMO remains robust beyond Semiconductors. Analysts
expect Q3 earnings growth of 30.7% y/y, with positive growth across all 11

sectors (chart).

### Page 9

Our diffusion indexes also show that an increasing share of S&P 500
companies is experiencing rising forward revenues and earnings (chart).
Combined with expectations that semiconductor demand will continue to
outpace supply, at least over the near term, this suggests that FEMO
remains fundamentally rational.

SUMMARY: AI euphoria meets macro headwinds
Tech remained a major driver of equity market performance in Q3. The
Mag 7 outperformed the broader market while the S&P 500 also

posted gains, lifted by mega-cap and tech stocks.
Still, tech’s path in Q3 was volatile. July began with a reversal of the AI
trade as investors questioned elevated valuations, the sustainability of
AI spending and the outlook for semiconductor demand. That led to a
large sell-off in chip stocks, with large falls in Q2 outperformers SK
Hynix and Samsung contributing to a sharp decline in Korea’s KOSPI.
The Philly SOX experienced its worst monthly performance since

2008.
By August, however, sentiment had improved. The S&P 500 reached
fresh record highs, even as US Treasury yields rose, supported by
stronger cloud earnings and signs of AI monetization. September
proved more challenging as higher oil prices, inflation concerns and
rising bond yields weighed on risk assets, particularly semiconductors.
The Fed also raised its policy rate by 25bp on Sep 16.
Yet, despite these macro headwinds, company guidance continued to

indicate strong AI infrastructure demand, with Micron’s stronger-than-
expected revenue guidance on Sep 30 providing further evidence of
robust demand for memory chips. Memory supply remains tight,
semiconductor capex is accelerating, and the industry’s attention has
increasingly turned to bottlenecks in optical networking and photonics,
as well as concerns around AI regulation and safety. Looking ahead,
tech is likely to remain an important driver of equity returns into Q4,

although elevated valuations and tighter financial conditions leave the
sector sensitive to earnings disappointments. Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Updated Sep 30. Returns in local currency.
Q3 started with doubts about the AI trade but ended with renewed confidence

1. GLOBAL INDUSTRY BREAKDOWN
Tech hardware, including Lenovo, Advantech, and HP Inc, rebounded in Q3. This was partly driven by accelerating AI

server demand and the ongoing data center build-out, although continuing memory shortages raised costs for
hardware manufacturers. Cloud and server providers, like Oracle, Dell and HPE, also highlighted strong AI-related
demand. Semis and equipment were the weakest performers. Elsewhere, entertainment and telecoms posted modest
declines. Finally, in Q3 investor attention increasingly shifted to AI agents, with Meta’s Muse, Microsoft’s Copilot and

Salesforce’s Agentforce among the prominent offerings.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Median returns taken. Updated Sep 30.

2. SEMICONDUCTORS
Chip stocks were hurt initially by a reversal of the AI trade and then by rising bond yields. The Philly SOX index fell
sharply in Q3, after its worst monthly performance in July (-21%) since 2008. Concerns about higher oil prices and
rising rates continued to weigh on the sector in Aug and Sep. SK Hynix was among the weakest performers, amid an

earnings disappointment relative to expectations and an unwinding of leveraged positions. Its Nasdaq ADRs began
trading in July. Despite these headwinds, the semis outlook remains supported by AI infrastructure demand. Within
semis, potential long-term beneficiaries include suppliers of AI infrastructure, memory including high-bandwidth
memory (HBM), advanced packaging, photonics and semiconductor equipment.

On July 30, Microsoft rose more than 15%, its strongest daily gain since 2008, following results released the previous

evening. The move added ~$450bn to its market cap, the largest one-day increase on record at the time. For context,
that daily increase is larger than the value of any company in Europe (except ASML).

3.2 MAGNIFICENT 7: Market concentration
According to our equity strategists, from end-July to mid-September, tech-exposed stocks within the S&P 500 rose

exposed sector. In late July, our strategists anticipated a rotation back into technology, which remains underway.

4. REGIONAL BREAKDOWN
After outperforming in Q2, mainland Chinese tech stocks posted large declines this quarter, hurt by July’s sell-off.
The CSI 300 tech series led the declines. Weakness also extended across Asia more broadly, with the KOSPI, Nikkei 225
and Hang Seng Tech down in Q3, driven by a chip sell-off and concerns over further Fed hikes. European tech equities
were more resilient, with the STOXX Europe 600 Tech Index down slightly lower in Q3 but still well above its year-end

level. Within European tech, Infineon underperformed, while Orange illustrated weakness in telecoms outside the index.

Document 363: Sales Trader Commentary_ TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally_ _ GSTW

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sales Trader Commentary_ TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally_ _ GSTW.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-13

Status: ok

### Page 1

Sales Trader Commentary: TAIEX 49k Wobble -
July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa
Rally? | GSTW
13 October 2026 | 4:11 PM Taiwan Standard Time
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
Index lost the 49k points threshold and saw the biggest decline on a point basis since
the end of July. Market seemed to lose the steam to break through 50k points after
the past choppy sessions. Is this July de ja vu or some healthy pullback? Foreigners

and domestic investors seem to share different narrative. Check out below for our
investor feedback.
Key Investor Feedback and Observations
Positioning. we saw meaningful outflows today from all kinds of investors today,
again focusing in ex-TSMC large caps. Today foreigners net sold US$5.2bn today,
the second largest in the history (simultaneously drove TWD weakness over 32.23
today). Domestic investors, especially retail, on the other hand, anchored the

market with the record high margin loan balance (as of 10/12/2026). Local funds
remained buyer as well mainly thanks to ETFs inflow.
Macro. CPI headline brought uncertainty and forced global investors to turn
cautious, which was exacerbated by high yields and crude. For Taiwan, AI trade is
relatively resilient to the Middle Eastern tension and high energy prices. However,
at some point, it is going to have a real economic impact either through Fed or

some other macro data.
Sentiment. Surprisingly, people are still bullish in Taiwan, though they also
acknowledge some choppy trading sessions ahead. Local investor feedback is not
calling for capitulation; in fact, most believe this is just another "two-steps forward
one-step backward" cadence towards another record high. Nonetheless, some
retail start taking chips off the table. This echoes our observation among the
foreign investors. Yes, the AI fundamental future is all sunshine, but as we head into

the end of year, it is natural for them to protect their great fun thus far this year
instead of adding on immediate risk. I'd imagine the next few weeks are going to be
full of 1) rapid sector rotation; 2) weak or unsustainable momentum before Santa
comes into town after the midterm election for our year-end rally Christmas gift.
TSMC Earnings and Others. We know the numbers are going to be good, and we
also know people are going to sell-on-facts after TSMC earnings call; recall most

recent one happened right after the meeting that scarred a lot of investors since.
Perhaps this time people started trimming ahead (and maybe too early?), which
provides some window for a quick rebound into small and mid cap. OCP summit is
happening right now, and TPCA Show will start next week. Watch out for single
stock movement mentioned in the conferences.
10/13/26, 4:13 PM Sales Trader Commentary: TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally? | GSTW

https://marquee.gs.com/s/content/markets/812c043a-8535-4003-887e-b01f79096974 1/8

Sector. 1) ABF/CCL is still the crowded long sectors for both foreign and domestic
investor, but echoing on above points, investors need more catalysts to add on
risks or drive the re-ratings. 2) Power is another big issues that data centers need
to resolve, power solutions and fuel cells name are also key focus in Taiwan. 3) Let's
not forget about OSATs. We need more equipment and testing capacity to suffice
enlarging demand from both ASIC and GPU.

Key Stocks I Follow: Unimicron, Kinsus, NYPCB, ZDT, EMC, TUC, Delta, Lite-On,
Kaori, ASE, KYEC, All Ring
Risk. Upside looks thin in the short term. Good earnings are not enough - look at
Nanya Tech and Memory sector today. Chasing momentum now may get hurt. I also
smelled some short unwind today (i.e. funding short names outperformance such
as Realtek, Novatek, Shipper, etc) - signs of de-leveraging. Directionless is how I
would describe the past few days, but maybe it's time to find some value Buy?

performance) of their underlying index or benchmark during the same period of time. This effect can be magnified in volatile markets.

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer

Portfolio Strategist

Islamic finance is growing at a rapid pace, driven 
primarily by increasing demand for investors who 
seek Shariah -compliant investments. Sukuk, often 
referred to as Islamic bonds, surpassed USD 1trn in 
2025, making them one of the fastest -growing 
segments o f Islamic finance. The asset class’s 
unique value proposition is driving its popularity, 
especially in an increasingly volatile global  
economic landscape. 
In this publication, we will explore the evolving role of Sukuk in wealth management, 
examining market fundamentals, risk frameworks and emerging opportunities within 
this market.

### Page 3

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  3

Sukuk, commonly referred to as Islamic bonds, are Shariah-compliant investment 
certificates representing ownership interests in underlying assets, usufructs (the 
right to use an asset and receive its benefits, without owning it) or ventures. In 
return, Sukuk holders receive periodic distributions, typically in the form of rental 
payments (Ijarah), profit-sharing (Mudarabah) or asset-generated cash flows, and 
upon maturity the principal amount is repaid. Sukuk differ from conventional 
bonds in several ways: 
Fig. 1  
Sukuk vs. conventional bonds

Aspect  Conventional bonds Sukuk

Core principle  Lending (time-value of money) Asset ownership 
Underlying asset  Any asset, business or project Must be Sharia-compliant 
Returns  Guaranteed (fixed or floating interest) Not legally guaranteed (depends on 
underlying asset’s performance) 
Risk   Issuer bears all performance risk Shared risk between parties 
Transparency  Moderate (depends on disclosure) High (asset identification required)

Source: Standard Chartered

The role of Sukuk in the investing landscape is evolving amid 
global uncertainty and rising geopolitical tensions. Investors 
are increasingly seeking more stable and asset ‑backed 
alternatives. An investor can consider investing in Sukuk for 
the following reasons: 
Resilient 
Sukuk provide asset -backed collateral that conventional 
bonds often lack, offering a cushion during volatile market 
conditions. Historically, Islamic investments, including Sukuk’, 
have been countercyclical, i.e. they tend to expand or remain 
stable when c onventional markets contract. Across major 
market stress from the 2018 US-China Trade War through the 
2026 Middle East Conflict, Sukuk bonds consistently 
outperformed Global Bonds — delivering smaller drawdowns 
in risk-off episodes such as the 2018 US-China trade war and 
2022 higher for longer inflation, and greater resilience in 
geopolitical oil shocks, underscoring the asset class's 
structural defensive characteristics.

1 The evolving role of Sukuk

Strong fundamentals
The Sukuk market experienced an unprecedented 25% growth in issuance in 2025, with issuance nearly USD 300bn. According
to some  reports,  the  Sukuk  market  could  reach  USD  3trn  by  2030  driven  by  increased  demand  for  Shariah-compliant
investments, sovereign funds’ diversification needs, development of regulatory frameworks for Sukuk and growth in Middle East

capital markets.  Nearly  80%  of  Fitch ‑rated Sukuk  carry  investment ‑grade ratings  and  have  little  or  no  history  of  default,
underscoring the sector’s strong fundamentals. These factors should help to instil confidence among investors, who can look at
Sukuk as central building blocks of their portfolio.

Convergence with sustainable investing trends
Sukuk and sustainable bonds share more common ground than their different origins suggest, making sustainable Sukuk a
natural bridge between Islamic finance and sustainable investing. This also helps explain Sukuk’s growing appeal beyond
traditional Muslim investors, as evidenced by Sustainable Sukuk issuance rising from USD 464mn in 2017 to USD 9bn in 2024,

representing a CAGR of 52.7%. The overlap starts with what Sukuk exclude: Shariah principles prohibit interest, excessive
uncertainty and speculation, and screen out sectors financing harmful industries such as tobacco, gambling, weapons, adult
entertainment and alcohol – a negative screen that closely aligns with the exclusionary (negative screening) method, commonly
used in sustainable investing. Tradi tional Sukuk bonds require proceeds to be linked to identifiable real assets or projects,

creating a ring-fenced structure like use-of-proceeds sustainable bonds. Combined with Shariah’s broader objectives of social
welfare, resource stewardship and equita ble risk-sharing, this creates strong alignment with the United Nations Sustainable
Development Goals.
Green Sukuk is an established example, where proceeds are earmarked for environmental projects such as renewable energy,
clean transport, energy-efficient buildings or sustainable water and wastewater management. Issuers must meet both Shariah

approval from a recognised scholarly board and alignment with International Capital Market Association (ICMA) Green Bond
Principles.
Traditional Sukuk already comply with Shariah laws such as avoiding Riba (interest -based returns arising from lending
transactions), Gharar (investments with ambiguity) and Maysir (speculation). Sustainable Sukuk build on this foundation by
adding environmental and social objectives. The table below highlights how sustainable Sukuk differ from traditional Sukuk.

Digital integration
The integration  of  blockchain  technology  and  digital  platforms  is  revolutionising  Sukuk  issuance  and  trading,  dramatically
reducing transaction costs and expanding accessibility to  a broader  investor base.  For instance, SAKKEX (an  independent
blockchain-based Sukuk marketplace) streamlines drafting, issuance, and near ‑instant settlement. Its Sukuk Wallet App also
allows investors to trade on the go. Several such digital platforms now allow tokenised Sukuk that enable fractional ownership,

automated Shariah  compliance  verification  and  real-time  settlement,  thereby  addressing  challenges  that  have  persisted  in
Islamic finance markets. Digital tools also simplify cross border Sukuk transactions by enabling end to end digital origination and
trading, with smart contracts automating profit sharing to cut operational costs and enhance transparency.

### Page 6

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026 6
Sukuk structures define the nature of ownership, risk -sharing and return
generation. Several types of Sukuk structures exist and are defined by the
underlying Shariah contract. We discuss some prominent ones below and see
how they align with various phases of an investor’s wealth journey:
Sukuk al-Ijarah is a Shariah-compliant lease-based bond where the investor gains partial ownership in a physical

asset such as a building, airport or equipment. The asset is then leased back to the issuer who pays a nominal
(pre-agreed) rent to the investor. An investor seeking stable, predictable income streams with lower volatility can
consider investing in these Sukuk. The original principal amount is returned at the end of the lease period.
Ijarah Sukuk are ideal for the wealth preservation phase of an investor’s wealth journey. For instance, investors
nearing retirement typically need to restructure their portfolios by shifting from growth to preservation while

generating steady income to cover living expenses. Ijarah Sukuk deliver on both fronts: protecting capital through
asset-backed structures while providing predictable rental income that supports household needs without
depleting principal.
Sukuk al-Murabahah are cost-plus financing structures where the issuer buys an asset and immediately resells
it to the investors at a marked-up price, with payment deferred over a pre-determined time. Consider this: Let us

assume, a company needs USD 100m. A special purpose vehicle (SPV), established by the company, mobilises
funds from investors and buys commodities worth USD 100m and immediately resells them to the company for
USD 112m payable over time. The USD 12m difference is the agreed profit margin. Murabahah Sukuk ar e best
suited for investors seeking income over a short to medium term, typically, 1-5 years. Investments in this type of
Sukuk return the principal gradually throughout the investment period rather than only at maturity.

Investments in Murabahah Sukuk are best suited for wealth protection and income generation. Unlike Ijarah which
return principal as a lump sum at maturity, Murabahah amortise both profit and principal in each instalment. This
makes them ideal for retirees who need to systematically draw down capital to fund living expenses without
depleting their portfolio too quickly.
Sukuk al-Mudarabah is a profit-sharing partnership arrangement where investors provide 100% of the capital

and operate as silent partners. The issuer manages the business or project as the active partner. Under a
Mudarabah arrangement, financial losses arising from normal bu siness risks are borne by the investors in
proportion to their capital contribution, while the Mudarib loses its effort and entitlement to profit. However, where
losses result from negligence, misconduct, breach of agreed terms, or misconduct by the Mudari b, the Mudarib
may be held liable. Think of it as venture capital meets bonds; you fund the business, professionals run it and

you share the profits or bear the losses resulting from the business activities. Mudarabah Sukuk are often
regarded as one of the  structures most closely aligned with the Shariah principle of profit and loss sharing.
However, they bear higher risk and lower predictability compared to other structures.
Investors with a growth mindset and open to high -risk tolerance can consider investing in these Sukuk . They
best align with accumulation and growth phase of the wealth journey, typically, when investors have long time

horizons to absorb volatility and can afford capital risk for higher returns. However, to balance risk, it is important
for investors to diversify across various types of Sukuk. Even within Mudarabah, they can consider investing
in multiple projects, sectors and geographies.
1 Sukuk structures and
wealth mandates

### Page 7

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  7

A joint venture partnership structure where both investors and the issuer contribute capital is Sukuk al -
Musharakah. In this structure, both parties (issuer and investors) jointly provide capital, participate in managing 
the venture, and share profits or losses based on agreed upon ratios. This structure appeals to investors focused 
on growth, interested in active participation, and prepared to take on greater risk in pursuit of higher returns. 
Musharakah Sukuk are best suited for the growth and accumulation phase where investors are building wealth 
aggressively, comfortable with high risk and able to lock up capital for the long term (typically 5+ years).

Sukuk al-Wakalah is an agency arrangement where investors appoint a representative from the issuing company 
as their Wakil (agent) to invest their funds in specific Shariah-compliant assets or projects in exchange for a fee. 
This arrangement is similar to a mutual fund or ETF, where the agent manages a diversified pool of Shariah 
compliant assets on behalf of the investors. Investors benefit from professional fund management, diversification, 
transparency and flexibility. Depending on issuer preferences, Wakalah Sukuk can b e structured with lower 
investment thresholds, making them accessible to a wide range of investors. 
Wakalah Sukuk suitability depends heavily on the underlying asset allocation and risk profile. Conservative 
Wakalah structures investing in stable, income -generating assets suit wealth preservation. Moderate -risk 
Wakalah can complement accumulation portfolios as a diversification tool, though not as a primary growth driver 
as their structure and risk mandate limit upside potential.

Sukuk al-Istisna is a project financing structure based on a manufacturing or construction contract. Investors 
provide upfront capital to finance the construction of specific assets. Upon completion, the asset is sold to an end 
buyer (often the original issuer or a third party) who makes deferred payments including the cost plus a mark-up, 
which are then distributed to investors. Istisna suit seasoned investors with construction finance expertise and 
those who can absorb development risk and commit capital for the long term. Owing to its complexity and higher 
risk profile, this type of Sukuk typically requires large initial investments, making it less accessible to retail 
investors.  
In the wealth cycle, Istisna are best aligned with the accumulation and growth phase, when long time horizons 
can absorb construction delays and development risks. The long-term commitment and potential for cost overruns 
or project failure make it unsuitable for later wealth stages such as retirement.

Sukuk al -Salam is an Islamic bond backed by a forward purchase contract where investors pay upfront for 
commodities to be delivered later. At its core, they are similar to forward contracts. However, unlike a forward 
contract, Salam contracts are fully prepaid, standardi sed and designed with clearly defined delivery terms to 
reduce uncertainty (gharar). Under this arrangement, an investor profits from the difference between the lower 
Salam purchase price (paid today) and the higher resale price (received at delivery).  
Salam Sukuk carry high delivery and price risk and aligns well with investors in the growth and accumulation 
phase. Salam requires strong commodity‑market expertise, deep understanding of production and delivery risks, 
and the ability to assess the issuer’s operational capacity to deliver the specified goods on time.

considered relatively less risky than conventional bonds. However, they are not
immune to risks. Unique challenges such as asset quality deterioration and
structure-specific exposures require rigorous risk management.

Due diligence in Sukuk extends beyond traditional credit
analysis and an investor should also consider the following:
• Shariah compliance verification can be done by evaluating

the Sukuk structure and confirming that its mandates and
underlying assets fully align with Islamic principles.
• Evaluating asset quality by verifying tangibility, cash -
generating capability and legal ownership can help
investor reduce risk.
• Assessing creditworthiness and market depth will help in
identifying financially strong Sukuk issuers and ensuring
adequate liquidity.
• Cross-border Sukuk investments remain viable,
contingent on careful scrutiny of legal documentation

across multiple jurisdictions to ensure enforceability and
investor protection.
Robust due diligence and proactive risk mitigation strategies
are essential to protect capital and stay aligned with Islamic
financial principles.
1 Risk management and due diligence

### Page 10

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  10

Staying up to date on the latest developments in 
the Sukuk market is important as this space is 
constantly evolving and rapidly expanding. 
Moreover, Islamic finance is not static; Shariah 
interpretations related to Sukuk structures are 
periodically redefined. Staying informed protects 
investors from investing in non -compliant 
options. We look at some of the factors that are 
guiding the future of this landscape:

1 Emerging trends

Green Sukuk issuance is projected to grow materially through 
2030, reflecting the natural convergence of Islamic finance and 
sustainability principles. Key sectors include renewable 
energy, green buildings, low -carbon transportation, water and 
waste management and sustainable agriculture.

Tech advancements  are revolutionising Sukuk origination, 
trading and accessibility. Digitisation offers numerous benefits 
to the Islamic finance landscape: streamlined issuance and 
trading processes, enhanced accessibility through 
tokenisation, immutable audit trails via bl ockchain technology 
and improved secondary market liquidity.

A major regulatory shift is likely to create standardised rules 
across Islamic finance markets globally. The pivotal 
development is AAOIFI's Standard 62, launching in 2026, 
which mandates transparent, Shariah-compliant asset-backing 
and genuine asset ownership. This can fundament ally 
strengthen the Islamic authenticity of Sukuk structures.

The Sukuk market is rapidly expanding beyond its traditional 
Islamic finance strongholds. Non-traditional territories  in 
Africa (e.g., Algeria, Benin and Nigeria), Europe (e.g., 
Luxemburg, UK) and Asia (e.g., Turkey, Pakistan) are 
emerging as Islamic capital market gateways. This expansion 
underscores the evolution of Sukuk from a niche Islamic 
product to a globally reco gnised asset class attracting diverse 
sovereigns and investors across continents.

### Page 11

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  11

Conclusion

Ultimately, Sukuk demonstrate that Shariah compliance and sound investment 
strategy are not competing priorities, but complementary ones. By ensuring that 
portfolios are grounded in real economic activity by offering stability through 
tangible asset -backing and transparent structures that channel capital towards 
productive use. Their foundation in ethical, responsible financing appeals to 
investors seeking both resilience and purpose. As global markets increasingly 
prioritise integrity and long-term sustainability, Sukuk stand out as a compelling tool 
for Shariah -compliant investors who want meaningful diversification without 
compromising on their religious and ethical principles.

### Page 22

全球生產基地加速擴產，效益將於2026年起逐步顯現
◼ 泰國一廠：
◼ 主要生產高階iHDI、HLC及光模塊產品，於3Q25進入量產。
◼ 量產爬坡順利，已通過多家客戶認證及量產，尚有多家伺服器與光模塊全球領先客戶正積極進行認證中。
◼ 7月已達損益兩平。
◼ 目前另有三棟新廠房及一棟機械鑽孔中心同步建置中，規劃將於4Q26起逐步進入試產階段。
◼ 淮安園區：
◼ 規劃至2026年底，淮安HA、HB、HC園區將有6棟高階PCB新廠完成建設。
◼ 另HD園區已於今年四月動土，規劃新建HDI/MSAP與HLC廠，8月10日封頂，預計明年三月加入生產。
◼ 深圳園區：
◼ 第三園區智慧製造基地於今年七月動土，預計興建三棟智慧製造廠房，重點布局AI伺服器用及光模塊用的
高階類載板，及AI終端用高階軟板等高端產品。
◼ 禮鼎深圳第二園區於今年九月正式動土，總投資金額不低於人民幣120億元，規劃建設數座工廠，聚焦
FCBGA等高階IC載板。新產能主要面向AI、高效能運算及先進封裝需求，進一步強化公司高階FCBGA產品的

研發、製造與規模化交付能力。

致力推動EPS＋ESG，國際ESG評比持續提升
► 標普全球企業永續評鑑 S&P Global ESG
2025年S&P Global ESG評級進步至88分，並入選道瓊領先世界
指數 (DJBIC World) 及道瓊領先新興市場指數 (DJBIC Emerging
Markets)，亦於2022年至2025年連續4年入選標普全球永續年

高利率環境不利於公用事業評價
分析師／謝孟璇
研究範疇／公用事業/汽車/科技
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《市場預估聯準會年底再升息一碼 ，不利公用事業
獲利與評價》
聯準會在 9月FOMC會議決議升息一碼 ，使美國目
標 利 率 區 間 上 升 至3.75~4% ， 且 經 濟 預 估 摘 要
( SEP )顯示年底前將再升息一碼；另外，受累於近
期美伊陷入外交僵局 ，投資者對於衝突長期化的擔
憂推升了國際油價 ，導致做為全球無風險利率定價
基準的10年期美債殖利率攀升至5.2%附近。公用事
業屬於資本密集型產業 ，極度依賴債務融資來支持
基礎設施建設與更新 ，升息提高了企業利息支出 、
直接壓縮利潤空間 ，對公用事業獲利與產業評價相

對不利。此外，過去兩、三年市場為電力公用事業
公司注入AI溢價，導致板塊估值偏高 ，預估短期內
公用事業股價大幅向上的機率較低 。紐約州、德州、
賓州相繼對大型資料中心新建案祭出暫停令或更嚴
格的電網接入限制 ，部分對資料中心建設較保守州
的公用事業公司長期營收成長可能減緩。

中國兩年期殖利率 1.25% 景氣下行壓力持續，本行仍預期接下來有降準50bp空間。
新興市場股 中立 油價高檔拉鋸，主要國家央行貨幣政策偏向緊縮，債務堆壘隱憂揮之不去，高利環境下，兼具基本面穩健與政策操作有空間的新亞、拉美相對正向。
新興亞洲 中立 新興亞洲中相對看好台灣、韓國、東協與印度，AI推論的供不應求，適時留意評價面空間循序參與台韓投資契機，印度有評價面保護優勢。

中國A 中立 中國景氣K型化加劇，投資方向維持選股不選市，聚焦受惠AI相關供應鏈兼國產替代政策扶植的科技族群、稀缺原物料及高附加出口價值等產業。
香港 中立 削價競爭雖告尾聲但內需乏力影響獲利，並關注資本支出轉為獲利進程。
台灣 正向 長線台股AI供應鏈握技術進程優勢與漲價潮，擇評價面分批布局台股。

重點摘要
3
• 8月中國經濟數據仍顯示
外貿強 ，內需疲軟的景
象。除反應信心不足 、
內需疲軟外 ，反內卷的
制約以及政策傾斜高端
科技是主因。
• 中國經濟現階段來自價
格為主的出口貢獻 ，景
氣越趨依賴全球科技週
期，內需疲軟將視財政
執行力道進程才能緩解
接下來的景氣下行壓力 。
《投資關鍵-中國經濟K型化情勢依舊》
縱觀8月中國經濟數據仍顯示外貿強，內需疲軟的景
象。中國8月出口增速從前月23.9%回升至25%，究
其細項，科技相關以名目計算年增速續保高速 ，如
自動資料處理機器零件年增76.5%， 儲存組件年增
159.2%，印刷電路板年增 44.4%與積體電路年增
129.8%。惟若以數量計算年增大多回落，顯示科技

8月出口增速佳來自價格貢獻。另一留意低端消費出
口也見改善，可觀察歐美消費旺季的挹注力道。
8月零售銷售持續疲軟年增回落至 0.4%，汽車零售
年衰退幅度擴大至-18.5%是主要拖累，零組件價格
上揚帶動通訊設備零售增27.3%。
8月實質工業生產年增略好預期至 5.2%，科技相關
保持較高能見度 ，通訊、電腦及其他電子設備年增
17.2%，但如汽車 、粗鋼與水泥較反應內需表現黯
淡，反內卷政策制約太陽能板等生產表現 。固定資
產投資年衰退幅度擴大至 -7.2%，房地產投資雙位
數衰退趨勢不變 ，製造業投資年減 -2.3%，我們認
為除反應信心不足 、內需疲軟外 ，反內卷的制約以
及政策傾斜高端科技是主因。
總體來看，中國經濟現階段來自價格為主的出口貢
獻，景氣越趨依賴全球科技週期 ，內需疲軟將視財
政執行力道進程才能緩解接下來的景氣下行壓力。
出口雖穩健，但內需與投資乏力，產能利用不振

《投資關鍵-台灣連十季利率不動 ，但升息壓力增 》
台灣央行第三季理監事會議宣布重貼現率、擔保放
款融通利率與短期融通利率維持不變在 2.0%、
2.375%和4.25%；可轉讓定期存單 (NCD) 28天期、
91 天期 、 182 天 期 也 維 持1.215% 、 1.275% 和
1.395% 不變。2024年以來，台灣央行持續保持利
率十連凍紀錄。
不動產信用管制部分 ，由於截至7月底，全體銀行
不動產貸款集中度續降至34.44%，低於2020年12

月底(第1度調整信用管制時點 )之 36.64%)，因此
調升自然人第 2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限至 7成。
同時也刪除購地貸款有關切結於一定期間內動工興
建之規定，相關規範回歸銀行依授信實務辦理。
資料來源：台灣央行，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09/18。

重點摘要
6
• 本行認為台灣景氣動能佳
且增長更加均衡 ，形成經
濟的正向循環 ，台灣經濟
環境已有宏觀的升息條件 。
年以來相較各國重返寬鬆貨幣政策列車 ，台灣依
舊維持基準利率 2%，2024/7各類新台幣存款準
備率調升 1碼。另觀察台灣 7月M2年增速 7.42%
(今年以來6.6%)雖高於政策目標區間 2.5%~6.5%，
但低於17.8%的名目 GDP 增長率，意味貨幣需求
增長速度超過貨幣供應量 ，因此台灣貨幣政策環
境已偏緊縮，所以並不需要立即升息。
展望後市，隨AI應用擴散至推論 、代理及終端裝
置推升算力需求 ，AI投資建設的暢旺驅動相關硬
體升級與用量成長 ，台灣企業涵蓋重要 AI供應鏈
節點，強勁出口引領經濟成長。

觀察AI科技紅利也逐步外溢其他產業 ，非科技如
機械出口逐步加溫 。相關半導體投資擴產需求積
極，投資成長也助力台灣經濟 ，內需也因股市財
富效應帶動。台灣景氣動能佳且增長更加均衡 ，
形成經濟的正向循環。
資料來源：主計處，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/08/07。

並非主要來自供給測壓力，主要是考量核心CPI 已
連續四個月維持在 2%以上，搭配民間消費對 GDP

的貢獻逐季顯現 ，這表明持續的內需復甦正在產

長表示 ，普發現金經費已納入總預算 ，立法院預
算審查進度將定奪普發現金 1萬的時點，目前可能
的時間最快在2027農曆春節（2月4日至10日）前
後，抑或在元宵節（2月20日）前。而根據央行預
估1Q27整體物價增速仍高於 2%，若疊加現金補
貼的加持 ，不排除物價增幅將更高於原預期水準 。
其次央行聲明第二個觀察重點 : 主要經濟體貨幣政
策緊縮程度。美歐日央行9月已升息一碼，美聯準
會年底尚有升息一碼的可能性 ，韓國央行 7、8月
連續性升息後，11月與1Q27都有可能再度升息。
多國相繼貨幣政策緊縮態勢 ，或增加台灣央行貨
幣政策抉擇的壓力 。而若台灣基準利率與他國利
差擴大 ，也可能引發資金外流 ，也不利央行關心
的金融情勢發展 。因此綜合來看 ，本行預期 12月
央行理監事會議有可能決議升息半碼至2.125%。
《投資關鍵-聚焦穩健基本面有望抵禦高利環境》
展望後市，美財政部有意擴大美債回購規模緩和債

務壓力，美聯準會9月升息一碼至3.75%-4.0%，年
底不排除還有一碼的升息空間，但從通膨與景氣預
估路徑來看，美聯準會的升息舉措並非典型的升息
循環，而是「不承諾路徑的風險管理式升息」。
回到對新興亞洲貨幣政策路徑來看，或許促使部分
新興亞洲央行政策光譜往緊縮端推移，但不改變原
具降息需求新亞國家的寬鬆方向，整體新亞國家貨
幣政策走向分歧。更重要的是決定新興亞洲貨幣政
策的考量點，如台灣與韓國主要是因應景氣回溫而
貨幣政策緊縮，但像無財政補貼物價致緊縮貨幣政
策國家如菲律賓，再者還有經濟成動能尚不穩固或
通膨可控，貨幣政策按兵不動的如中國、泰國等。

目前反應的是下載熱度與想像空間，但尚未有付費
轉換率數據。大方向來看，AI推論的供不應求將持
續推動AI基礎建設需求，建議適時留意評價面空間
循序參與台韓投資契機。
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09/24。

《運動鞋服Nike轉型成效慢，獲利展望不佳》
Nike1Q27財年(2026/6~2026/8)營收與每股盈餘
皆衰退。運動休閒系列、Jordan品牌和大中華區市

轉變時(品牌忠誠度降低與多品牌嘗試 )，如何收復

《2026 美年底消費總額預期創新高 ，逾半增長靠
「通膨」，電商與生成式AI成雙引擎》
近期已有多家研調機構針對美國2026年年底消費
旺季出具預測報告。整體來看，各大機構預測的銷
售總額皆創下歷史新高 ，但其背後的共同隱憂是
「通膨」，直言超過一半的名目增長率是由物價上
漲驅動。這使得消費者普遍展現「價格敏感、主動
尋找折扣、切換品牌以省錢」的價值導向行為。電
商增速(網購)依然大幅碾壓實體零售。所有機構也
都點出生成式AI在2026年成為主流購物工具。
資料來源：Mastercard， 2026/9/23，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/13

機構預測2026年底消費旺季
各家機構重點分述如下
Mastercard ： 預 期 假日消費成長近半因物價上漲 ，
今年主因能源與AI建設成本增加，去年則受關稅影
響。鑑於柴油價格持續高企可能推高零售商的運輸
成本，並對年底的整體消費價格構成上行壓力，因
此值得密切關注。高收入家庭因財富成長、增加自
由支配支出，成為近年消費成長的重要引領者， 然
而，受益於勞動市場穩定、薪資成長與健康資產負
債表，各收入群體亦持續穩定貢獻，消費仍由廣大
家庭共同支撐。
Adobe：預計今年假期季節將推出極具競爭力的折

扣，雖然消費者歷來都熱衷於利用節日促銷活動購
買禮物或添加最新電子產品，但許多人現在更傾向
於利用折扣囤積生活必需品。另，今年假期季節美
國零售網站的人工智慧流 量 將年增130%。預計感
恩節的增幅最大(年增159%)，其次是黑色星期五(
年成長 95%)和網路星期一(年增88%)。
德勤(Deloitte) ： 消費者仍然重視為親朋好友營造
特別的節日氛圍，同時也在精打細算地消費。為了
更好地利用每一分錢，觀察到各個收入階層的消費
者都在追求性價比，例如在不同品牌和零售商之間
切換，以及利用促銷活動來控制支出。

Document 478: サイバー攻撃による経済損失をどう考えるか ～一企業の被害から、マクロ的な経済損失となるリスク～

Source PDF: all report pdf/サイバー攻撃による経済損失をどう考えるか ～一企業の被害から、マクロ的な経済損失となるリスク～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年10 月 14 日

1/2 
サイバー攻撃による経済損失をどう考えるか 
～一企業の被害から、マクロ的な経済損失となるリスク～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  前田 和馬 
（℡：050-5473-3888）

情報漏洩を中心にサイバー攻撃による被害が国内外で目立っている。相次ぐサイバー攻撃には
AIが悪用された可能性が指摘されており、今後もこうした被害は拡大する懸念がある。サイバー
攻撃による経済的な影響は主に3 つ考えられる。 
 まず、被害による直接的な金銭損失であり、仮想通貨等の資産の流出、身代金の支払い、被害
者への補償金支払いなどが挙げられる。FBIが被害申告ベースでまとめた「Annual Internet 
Crime Report 2025」によると、米国を中心としたサイバー犯罪の被害総額（個人被害も含む）は
209 億ドルと近年飛躍的に拡大している。なお、こうした被害は基本的に所得移転であり、生産活
動を捕捉する国内総生産（GDP）に直接的な影響を与えるわけではない。 
 次に、サイバー攻撃を通じた企業活動への損害だ。例えばCSISとMcAfeeによる 2020 年の調査で
は、サイバー攻撃を受けた場合に業務は平均18 時間中断したと指摘する。サイバー攻撃は既存顧
客へのサービス提供や新たな顧客獲得活動などを抑制する懸念がある。これに加えて、企業のブ
ランド価値を毀損し、顧客は該当サービスの利用を当面ためらうかもしれない。 
 サイバー攻撃による経済活動への被害が一企業に留まるのであれば、競合他社が代替的にサー
ビスを提供する、或いはシステム復旧後に挽回生産などが生じることで、マクロ的な影響は限定
される。しかし、こうした被害が「交通インフラなど代替が難しい企業」、或いは「産業内の広範
な企業」に及ぶ場合、サイバー攻撃で損なわれた供給能力を補うことが難しく、マクロ経済全体
の損失へと波及する。AIを通じたサイバー攻撃の頻発は、従来のミクロ的な「一企業の被害」か
ら、マクロ的な「産業や経済全体の被害」に転じるリスクを高めている可能性がある。 
 最後に、サイバーセキュリティ対策の強化によるIT関連支出の増加であり、これはGDPを押し上
げる可能性が高い。例えば、2000 年問題（西暦が2000 年になるとコンピュータが誤作動を起こす
との懸念）の際のIT対策費用は全世界で3,000～6,000 億ドルと、1999 年における世界GDPの 0.9
～1.8%に達したとみられている。一方、攻撃をうける企業の防御対策よりも、AI脅威論による開
発抑制の動きが強まる場合、データセンター投資や株式市場の調整を通じて景気減速へと繋がる
懸念がある。なお、米国ではトランプ政権が対中競争力の観点からAI規制に消極的であるほか、
連邦議会におけるFrontier ActなどのAI規制法案も成立に至るかは不透明だ。

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

Document 480: スペインは解散総選挙へ

Source PDF: all report pdf/スペインは解散総選挙へ.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日

MURC Focus

スペインは解散総選挙へ

～右派連立政権が成立する見込み～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 解散総選挙に臨んだサンチェス首相 
スペインのペドロ・サンチェス首相が 10 月 5 日、議会を解散し、11 月 29 日に総選挙（定数 350、比例代表制）
を行うと発表した。住宅価格や家賃の高騰に対する政府の緊急政令案が、10 月 2 日に下院で否決されたことを
受けて、少数与党による政権運営の行き詰まりを意識した首相が、国民に信を問う決断をした形である 。ただし、
サンチェス首相が率いる中道左派の与党・社会労働党（PSOE）の支持率は低迷している（図表 1）。

もともと PSOE の人気は低迷しており、前回 2023 年 7 月の総選挙でも、ライバル政党である中道右派の国民
党（PP）が第一党となっていた。しかしサンチェス首相は他の左派政党や地域政党の協力を受けて組閣し、政権
を維持した経緯がある。しかし首相周辺の汚職疑惑などから PSOE の人気は回復せず、次期の総選挙では PP
と同根の右派政党である Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。 
図表 1. 主要政党の支持率調査 図表 2. スペインの住宅価格

ユーロ圏 スペイン
（2015年=100、季調済）
（年、四半期）
〇スペインのサンチェス首相が10月5日、議会を解散し、11月29日に総選挙を行うと発表した。次期の総選挙
では野党第一党の PP と同根の右派政党 Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。 
〇PP と Vox が総選挙で勝利すれば、2018年6月以来の政権交代が実現する。こうした右派回帰を民族主義
の台頭という流れに位置付ける向きもあるが、スペインの国内事情はもっと複雑である。 
〇仮に総選挙を経て PP と Vox の連立政権が成立しても、両派間ですり合わせるべき課題も多く、スペイン政
治が安定するとは限らない。ゆえに、当面の問題への対応も進まないだろう。

### Page 2

解散の直接的な理由となった住宅価格の問題であるが、住宅市況が底にあった 2015 年を基準（＝100）とす
る指数でその動きを確認すると、直近2026 年4～6 月期は201 と、ユーロ圏全体（159）を大きく上回る（図表2）。
特に 2023 年以降の上昇が目覚ましいが、堅調な景気や移民の急増を受けて住宅需要が高まったことがその主
因である。

2. 民族主義的な右派回帰とは異なる 
PP と Vox が総選挙で勝利すれば、2018 年 6 月以来の政権交代が実現する。こうした右派回帰を民族主義
の台頭という流れに位置付ける向きもあるが、スペインの 国内事情は複雑であるため、必ずしもその流れに合わ
ない点、留意すべきである。まず、そもそも PSOE の人気そのものが低下している。確かに景気は堅調だが、住
宅価格の急騰のような社会問題に関して、サンチェス政権は具体的な処方箋を提示できなかった。

総選挙を経て政権交代が実現し、PP が Vox と連立を組んだとしても、それを民族主義の台頭の表れと理解す
るよりも、一定の期間ごとに繰り返される左派から右派へのリバランスの流れとして理解するほうが適切だろう。 ま
たスペインの場合、右派間に対立関係があることにも注目する必要がある。地域ナショナリズムが強いスペインで
は地方分権を求める保守の力が強く、中央集権を主張する保守（つまり Vox）と対立関係にある。

中央集権を望む保守の一部に戦前・戦後のフランコ体制を理想化する向きがあることも、地域ナショナリズムと
の対立を生んでいる。地域ナショナリズム政党の中には、自らを保守と位置付けながらも、中央集権を望む保守と
の対立から、PSOE や他の左派政党（Podemos や Sumar）と歩調を合わせる動きもある。スペインの民族主義は
多様なため、同国の右派回帰を反欧州連合（EU）や反移民の文脈で捉えることはできない。

例えばスペイン南東部のカタルーニャ州では、伝統的に左派も右派も民族主義を掲げ、独立運動（あるいは高
度な自治の獲得のための運動）を展開しているが、同時に票を奪い合う関係にある。最近は左派の カタルーニャ
共和主義左翼（ERC）が劣勢である。一方、右派のカタルーニャ同盟（AC）が優勢となっており、政党支持率では
PSOE に次ぐ第 2 位となっている。

AC は右派だが独立を主張しており、中央集権を主張する Vox とは対立する。一方、カタルーニャ州でも独立
に同調しない有権者は数多く、そうした有権者は PSOE に投票する傾向が強い。カタルーニャ州はスペインでも
有数の産業地帯であるため、労働運動が盛んだったことから、伝統的に PSOE の支持が厚いためである。このよ
うに、スペイン政治はまだら模様であるため、右派回帰と民族主義を同一視することは不適切である。

### Page 3

3. 総選挙後の新政権も多難な船出となる見込み 
総選挙後にいかなる立場の新政権が成立しようとも、スペイン経済の先行きに対する懸念は残る 。確かに景気
は堅調を維持しているが、一方、冒頭で述べた住宅価格の高騰のような社会問題に対する妙薬はなかなか存在
しない。加えて、経済成長の次なる牽引役を見いだせていないという問題もある。例えばスペイン景気を牽引する
輸出だが、旅行サービスに支えられたサービスの輸出は好調でもモノの輸出は不調である（図表3）。

中南米からの移民による労働供給が潤沢とはいえ、グローバルな労働力不足やスペインの人口構成（純増で
はあるが移民流入に依存した増加）に鑑みれば、スペインでも生産性の向上は不可欠である。それに資する総固
定資本形成は、実体として建設投資に偏っており、機械投資や知的財産投資の規模は限定的である（図表 4）。
これらの投資を増やすためには国による産業政策が欠かせない。

具体的には、規制緩和や税制優遇といった措置である。これまではサンチェス首相率いる PSOE 政権が少数
与党だったということや、PSOE が中道左派であるため経済成長よりも所得分配を重視したこともあり、そうした設
備投資を後押しするような政策が必ずしも打ち出されたわけではなかった。では PP と Vox の連立政権に交代し
たことでそれが可能になるかどうかというと、その可能性も高くない。

なぜならば、PP と Vox の連立政権が強いイニシアチブの下で産業政策を“上から”推し進めようとすると、中央
集権的な色彩が強くなり、地域ナショナリズムとの間であつれきが生じる展開が予想されるためである。中央集権
志向を弱めるという現実的な判断を Vox ができるかどうかが鍵を握るが、同党にはもともと PP から右派色の濃い
グループが離脱して結党した経緯があるだけに、その余地がどの程度か定かではない。

現状では総選挙を経て PSOE から PP と Vox の連立へと政権が交代する公算が大きいが、両党ですり合わせ
るべき課題も多く、政治が安定するとは限らない。ゆえに、当面の問題への対応も進まないだろう。 
図表 3. GDP に占める輸出の割合 図表 4. GDP に占める固定資本形成の割合

十月 19, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  印刷電路板 ‧ 台灣

臻鼎-KY 
(4958.TW/4958 TT)

ABF 營收成長超前與 HDI 擴產加碼，2027 獲利上調 
重要訊息 
9 月營收達 263.6 億元，月增 27.5%、年增 36.1%；3Q26 營收 646.2 億
元，季增 33.4%、年增 36.4%，達成我們及市場 3Q26 預估之 98.8%、
101，符合預期。  
評論及分析  
ABF 載板營收進度超前，上調 2026-27 年佔比至 5.7%、10.3%。3Q26 在
漲價效應下與新產能開出，ABF 營收貢獻預估可望超越 BT，載板營收佔比
上調至 12%； 2026-27 年載板佔比上修至 11.2%、16.9%(原 9.6%、
13.6%)，ABF 營收預期開始超越 BT。 
加碼擴產中高階 HDI，HDI 伺服器增速大幅超越 HLC 伺服器。2027 年成長
重心偏向高階 HDI Server， 管理層將 2030 年前新廠數增加至 31 座，總廠
房數達 59 座，目前已開工廠房 22 座(11 座為 HDI 廠、7 座為載板)，其中
7 座已開始裝機，2 027 年開始試產到量產。  
載板漲價效應及切入 AWS T4 及 Google V9 伺服器，2027  AI 應用佔比上
調。我們預估在載板漲價效應 使營收進度優預期下， 3Q26 AI 應用(伺服器、
光模塊、載板 )佔比達 26%，目前光模塊營收進度維持 2027 倍增不變，取
決泰國、淮安新廠實際貢獻時間；AI 伺服器預估在輝達 (美) VR computing 
tray 目標 20% 以上市占率，而則在 AWS(美) T4、Google (美) V9 主板將切
入，綜上我們將 26-27 AI 應用佔比上修至 25%、42%。 
投資建議  
由於新廠產能集中 2H26 開出，可認列的營收與獲利有限， 2027 年則由新
廠完全貢獻、高階 HDI 放量及產品組合改善 下，帶動營收與獲利提升。 我們
上調 2027 獲利 13%，維持「增加持股」評等，目標價上調至 775 元，相
當於 2027 每股盈餘預估的 26 倍本益比。  
投資風險  
美系客戶智慧型手機銷售不如預期，競爭者技術追趕超越預期。

### Page 3

台灣 臻鼎-KY

十月 19, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 3

圖 4: 公司概況  圖 5: 應用比重 
臻鼎科技設立於 2006 年 6 月，為台灣第一大軟板供應商，主
要產品為硬式印刷電路板 (R-PCB)、軟性印刷電路板 (FPC)、高
密度連接板(HDI)與 IC 載板(IC Substrate)  、類載板(SLP)及軟硬
結合板及 COF，2025 年合併營收年增 6%達 1,825 億元，
2024 年營收應用佔比為行動通訊 64.2%、電腦消費 25.9%、
IC 載板 5.2%、汽車雷達 /基地台/伺服器/其他 4.7%。

ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

Document 586: 同行IPO集体退潮，Anthropic独闯11月中旬窗口：1.5万亿美元估值直面流动性大考

Source PDF: all report pdf/同行IPO集体退潮，Anthropic独闯11月中旬窗口：1.5万亿美元估值直面流动性大考.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

受宏观利率冲击与同类公司纷纷延期或撤回 IPO 影响，美股秋季科技上市潮⼏乎清空。
Anthropic 成为唯⼀坚持按计划推进上市的 AI 巨头，拟于 10 ⽉底公开招股说明书，并以⾼达
1.5 万亿美元的潜在估值直⾯机构认购与市场流动性的严格考验。
摘要：
原本热闹的秋季科技 IPO 档期已⼏近清空，仅剩 Anthropic ⼀家独撑局⾯。在 AI 基础设施板块遭
遇利率冲击、估值承压、同类公司纷纷延期或撤单的背景下，这家 AI 实验室仍坚持按计划推进上
市，以⾼达 1.5 万亿美元的潜在估值，直⾯当前市场的流动性考验。
据路透援引知情⼈⼠消息，Anthropic 计划最快于 10 ⽉底公开招股说明书，并于 11 ⽉中旬完成
IPO ，预计将于 11 ⽉ 9 ⽇当周启动⾯向机构投资者的路演。知情⼈⼠同时提⽰，上述计划及时间
节点仍可能发⽣变化。
与此同时， AI 基础设施及数据中⼼领域的多家同类公司已相继推迟上市。传数据中⼼运营商
Switch 、 Nscale 、软银旗下 SB Energy ，以及⿊⽯⽀持的 AirTrunk REIT 和英伟达⽀持的

Firmus ，或延期、或暂停、或撤回上市计划。近期 Oura 的 IPO 表现疲软，⻓期持股型机构投资
者参与寥寥，进⼀步加剧了市场对于新股发⾏需求深度的忧虑。
同⾏纷纷退场， Anthropic 坚持推进
原本颇为丰富的秋季科技 IPO ⽇历，如今已⼏乎被清空。据路透报道，数据中⼼运营商 Switch 和
AI 基础设施提供商 Nscale 均已将上市计划推迟⾄今年晚些时候。知情⼈⼠透露， Nscale 的延期
部分源于需要回应美国证券交易委员会（ SEC ）⼯作⼈员的审查意⻅。软银旗下 SB Energy 此前
也已宣布推迟上市。
⿊⽯⽀持的 AirTrunk REIT 和英伟达⽀持的 Firmus 则更进⼀步，已撤回或暂停各⾃的 IPO 计划。 9
⽉以来，市场波动加剧、利率持续⾛⾼，企业争取理想估值的难度显著上升。
Nscale 已于 9 ⽉公开提交 IPO 注册申请， Switch 则选择以保密⽅式提交⽂件。保密申报允许公司
在向公众披露财务及业务细节前，先⾏与监管机构完成注册⽂件的审阅⼯作。

利率上⾏叠加 AI ⽀出疑虑，拖累板块估值
此轮 IPO 退潮的背后，是利率上⾏与 AI 基础设施投资回报质疑的双重夹击。
利率⾛⾼推动 AI 运营商股票和债券遭到抛售， CoreWeave 股价承压明显，为潜在发⾏⼈定价提
供了负⾯参照。与此同时，市场对科技公司⼤规模数据中⼼建设能否实现⾜够回报的质疑持续
升温， AI 算⼒⽀出是否已过度超前于实际需求，成为机构投资者评估任何 AI 相关 IPO 时的核⼼问
题。
尽管如此，仍有⼤量⼤型投资者对 Anthropic 的前景保持乐观，将其视为获取 AI 核⼼资产敞⼝的
稀缺机会，认为⻓期投资者对这⼀赛道 " 不可忽视 " 。
营收⾼增，但账⾯亏损与承诺⽀出规模庞⼤
根据《华尔街⽇报》审阅的 S-1 招股说明书草案， Anthropic 2026 年上半年营收达 163 亿美元，
远超去年同期的 11 亿美元。但同期净亏损也从约 130 亿美元骤扩⾄ 807 亿美元。

动，并⾮等额现⾦流出。然⽽即便剔除这⼀⾮现⾦项⽬，经营层⾯的压⼒依然显著： 2026 年上

Document 637: 惠誉博华中资商业银行信用质量测试结果分布特征：测试样本增至548家，覆盖约96%的商业银行资产，IDR测试结果中位数为BBB+

Source PDF: all report pdf/惠誉博华中资商业银行信用质量测试结果分布特征：测试样本增至548家，覆盖约96%的商业银行资产，IDR测试结果中位数为BBB+.pdf
Report source: 惠誉博华信用评级
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

研究报告│  20 October 2026 fitchbohua.cn 1

金融机构 
商业银行

惠誉博华中资商业银行信用质量测试结果分布
特征 
测试样本增至 548 家，覆盖约 96%的商业银行资产，IDR 测试结果中位数为 BBB+

长期主体评级和生存力评级测试结果分布 
基于惠誉博华银行评级标准，依据市场公开披露信息，惠誉博华对
548 家中资商业银行信用质量进行了测试，本次测试样本较 2025
年净增加 41 家，增幅为 8.1%，进一步拓展了对中资银行信用质量
的观察范围。548 家样本中包含 6 家国有银行、12 家股份制银行、
86 家城商行、326 家农商行、7 家民营银行、68 家村镇银行和 43
家农村信用社 。此次纳入测试的商业银行合并资产规模约占中国
商业银行总资产规模的 96.2%(以 2025 年末数据计算)，资产规模
大于 1,000 亿元的中资商业银行中，除 32 家因未公开披露部分关
键信用评估 数据而无法纳入测试外，其余银行均纳入本次信用质
量测试范围。 
以下评级结果均为惠誉博华通过收集市场公开数据、并应用银行
评级标准测试得来，并不代表样本银行最终的信用评级结果，亦
不构成评级行动。如果未来通过受托评级行动或其他方式能够获
得样本银行更多数据和有效信息，届时惠誉博华将会根据最新且
更为充足的资料对相关银行进行评估，其测试结果可能出现变化。  
长期主体评级(LT IDR) 
长期主体评级是在结合生存力评级(VR)和支持评级，即政府支持评
级(GSR)/股东支持评级 (SSR)基础上根据 “孰高原则”而得出(评级
框架的详细说明请参见本文 附录评级框架部分 )。测试结果显示，
国有银行和绝大多数股份制银行的长期主体评级测试结果明显高
于其他类型银行，反映出这两类银行自身较强的抗风险能力，和
由更强系统重要性带来的更 高政府支持可能性。城商行和农商行
信用质量分化明显，优质的城商行和农商行长期主体评级测试结
果能够达到部分股份制银行的水平。整体观察，样本测试结果 中
枢为 BBB+，但目前市场中部分银行公开数据并不充分，尚有大量
小型银行未纳入测试范围，如果继续 扩大样本数量，惠誉博华预
计长期主体评级测试结果位于 BBB+以下银行数量将会明显上升。

数据来源：惠誉博华
家数
惠誉博华依据市场公开信息，对 548 家中资商业银行信用质量
进行 2026 年度测试，并对外公布全部样本的隐含长期主体评
级、隐含生存力评级及各项关键评级驱动因素的结果分布情
况。 惠誉博华持续扩大中资银行信用质量测试的样本覆盖范
围，纳入测试的机构数量由 2023 年的 413 家逐年增至 2024
年的 450 家、2025 年的 507 家及 2026 年的 548 家，较 2023
年累计增长 32.7%。样本覆盖的持续拓展，为观察不同类型银
行的信用质量分布及分化特征提供了更丰富的分析基础。 我们
力争在合理、透明、有区分度的评级原则下，最大程度上为投
资人、发行人和各类市场参与机构提供高水准的评级服务。 
最新测试结果显示，国有银行与多数股份制银行长期主体评级
测试结果明显高于其他类型银行。而城商行和农商行信用质量
呈现分化，优质城商行和农商行长期主体评级测试结果能够达
到部分股份制银行的水平。整体观察，样本机构的长期主体评
级隐含测试结果中枢落在 BBB+。

### Page 2

研究报告│  20 October 2026 fitchbohua.cn 2

金融机构 
商业银行

此次测试样本范围内的中资银行由生存力评级驱动的占比较高，
达到 83.2%。样本银行外部支持的主要类型为政府支持，惠誉博华
认为自身的经营状况和个体信用水平是大多数样本内的商业银行
信用质量的驱动因素。

另一方面，测试结果显示，在外部支持驱动的 79 家样本中资银行
中，54 家银行的隐含长期主体评级仅高于自身 VR “1-2”个子级，
占比 68.4%。

政府支持评级(GSR)与股东支持评级(SSR) 
惠誉博华通常根据最有可能的支持来源，评定银行 GSR 或 SSR。
支持评级反映了我们对特定银行在需要时获得外部支持力度和可
能性的看法，外部支持通常来自政府机构（含监管机构）或其股
东。惠誉博华根据政府或股东相关的因素评估其提供外部支持的
能力和意愿。 
政府支持评级(GSR) 
政府支持评级 主要反映 若银行在经营失败时，银行获得来自政府
方面的外部支持以避免其高级债务违约的可能性。当惠誉博华认
为受评主体受到来自政府的支持大于其股东支持，将授予银行政
府支持评级(GSR)。GSR 主要根据 8 项评估政府支持能力与意愿的
关键因素分析结果所决定。 
在评估政府支持评级时，惠誉博华的评估流程主要分为以下三步：  
• 第一步：确定政府支持的来源，以及相应层级政府的信用状
况。 
惠誉博华将评估商业银行对于不同层级政府的系统重要性，
确定最有可能获得支持的来源。通常情况下，对于中国监管
机构公布的国内系统重要性银行（ D-SIB），最有可能的外部
支持应为中央政府，此外部分非 D-SIB 但经过惠誉博华评估仍
对中国具有系统重要性的银行，亦有可能获得来自中央政府的
GSR。若惠誉博华认为特定商业银行系统重要性对中央政府较
低但对地方政府较高， 我们将评估来自于地方政府支持的可
能性，一般情况下认为能够提供地方政府支持的政府层级是
直辖市政府，省（或自治区）政府以及计划单列市政府。由
于地方政府间信用状况存在差异，惠誉博华亦对地方政府信
用状况进行评估。 
• 第二步：评估政府支持辖内银行业的能力和意愿。 
惠誉博华认为中国中央政府的信用状况为“极强”，对于银
行业的支持评级起点为“ aaa”。对于地方政府而言，惠誉博
华将通过 评估地方政府的信用状况以 确定地方政府支持辖内
银行的评级起点。在明确支持评级起点后，惠誉博华将评估
政府支持相应辖内银行业的能力和意愿，确定政府对辖内银
行业支持评级。 
由于受限于银行体系规模、结构以及相应政府的财务灵活性，
政府支持银行业的能力将有别于政府偿还自身债务的能力，
另外考虑到支持意愿方面亦或受限于处置立法和支持立场，
因此政府对辖内银行业支持评级将不高于相应政府自身的信
用状况。 
• 第三步：评估对于特定银行的 GSR。 
最后一步是确定对于特定银行的 GSR，在该阶段惠誉博华将
在政府对辖内银行业支持评级基础上，进一步评估政府对于
受评银行的支持意愿（或倾向），主要参考的 关键信用因素
包括系统重要性、负债结构以及股权结构。政府对于特定银
行的 GSR 将不高于政府对辖内银行业支持评级。 
本次测试我们共对 548 个样本中的 98 家商业银行进行了政府支持
评级评估，其中 21 家银行我们认为支持来源来自于中央政府，其
余 77 家银行支持来源分别来自于省、自治区、直辖市或计划单列
市政府。 
股东支持评级(SSR) 
惠誉博华 通过综合考虑股东的支持能力和意愿来确定股东支持评
级。股东支持评级的评估基于股东的长期主体评级，且通常与股
东提供支持的能力密切相关。之后，我们将在评估 10 项关键评级
驱动因素的基础上，基于股东长期主体评级进行调整得到最终 SSR。 
对于中资商业银行来说，受资产规模较高、系统重要性水平较强
等因素影响，绝大多数中资银行隐含政府支持评级高于银行股东
支持评级。 
生存力评级的关键驱动因素 (KRD)及测试结果
分布 
生存力评级反映的是不考虑外部支持情况下银行的自身经营状况
和个体信用质量。首先，惠誉博华应用矩阵映射的评估方法得出

经营环境的 隐含评分，并经过调整得到经营环境最终 评分1。其次，
在结合经营环境评分结果的基础上，评估其余 6 项关键评级驱动因
素(KRD)，包括 2 项定性因素(业务状况和风险状况)及 4 项财务状
况因素(资产质量、收益和盈利能力、资本和杠杆水平、融资和流
动性)。再次，通过已赋予固定权重的 6 项 KRD 加权平均计算得出
隐含生存力评级。由于经营环境对于评估其他 KRD 有很强的影响，
为了避免重复计算，不赋予经营环境单独的权重。 最后， 我们将
决定是否对于隐含生存力评级予以调整，并得出最终生存力评级
结果。在评级报告中，惠誉博华将同时披露隐含和最终生存力评
级，并在两者不一致时解释调整原因。

来源：惠誉博华 
本次测试结果显示，获得 aa 及以上生存力评级银行的数量明显少
于相同级别长期主体评级银行数量，这是由于部分获得较高长期
主体评级银行的评级驱动因素为政府支持评级。国有银行得益于
其最高的经营环境评分，良好的财务状况因素评分 (尤其是充足的
资本水平)，生存力评级测试结果显著优于其他类型银行；股份制
银行由于财务状况因素评分分化较大，生存力评级测试结果展现
出差异，其级别跨越 四个子级(notch)；城商行和农商行仍然分化
明显，但值得注意的是，一部分优质的城商行和农商行得益于财
务状况评分表现出众，生存力评级测试结果甚至优于部分股份制
银行，反映出其良好的自身经营状况。

1为区别不同类型评估结果，惠誉博华对直接影响受评主体信用质量的评估
结果(如 LT IDR、VR、GSR 和 SSR)使用“评级(Rating)”，对为得到“评
级”而进行的中间环节评估(如对 KRD 的评估)使用“评分(Score)”。 
经营环境 
经营环境评分是 评判银行生存力评级工作的基础部分。惠誉博华
通常会通过参考银行经营的区域情况和区域内的人均 GDP 共同组
成的矩阵映射得出经营环境隐含评分，并在此基础上结合其他维
度(例如区域经济稳定性、经济规模和结构 )进行调整并得出最终经
营环境评分。由于人均 GDP 通常与地区企业收益和家庭收入水平
密切相关，对于经营活动集中在一定区域的银行，人均 GDP 是经
营环境的有效指标。通常情况下，经营区域广泛且地域集中度较
低的银行，其经营环境评分较高。 
此次测试结果中，国有银行与股份制银行在实际经营区域上较其
他类型银行更具优势，业务的区域集中度很低，其经营环境评分
处于 aa 及以上类别。其他区域性商业银行中，部分主要经营地区
经济活力较好、产业结构多元的机构通常经营环境评分较高。整
体测试样本的经营环境中位评分为 a-。

业务状况 
银行的业务状况通常与其开展业务区域存在较大关联。因此，业
务状况隐含评分一般由银行经营环境评分和营业收入组成的矩阵
映射得出，并在此基础上根据业务模式、关键产品市场份额等因
素进行调整得出最终业务状况评分。国有银行、股份制银行经营
区域更加广泛，业务模式更加成熟，在市场上具有更强影响力，
其业务状况评分多数处于 aa 及以上水平。对比之下，测试范围内
存在大量样本机构业务状况评分处于 bb 水平，此部分机构通常经
营环境评分较为靠后，市场份额较小，收入稳定性更低。

风险状况 
风险状况评分通常反映了银行所面临的风险，以及风险管理措施
是否可以有效减少相应风险对其财务指标的影响。惠誉博华认为
银行的风险状况与其主要业务承担的风险关系密切，在评估商业
银行风险状况时，我们首先会评估银行的主要业务类型。对于主
要风险类型为信贷风险的银行而言，若投放集中于高风险行业、
或对某一地域较为集中，通常风险状况评分较低。若 风险状况评
分低于资产质量评分，可能表明当前授信标准较为宽松，其潜在
风险尚未充分体现在现有资产质量指标中 。测试结果显示，样本
内银行风险状况中位评分为 bbb+。

资产质量 
资产质量对于银行的债务偿还能力通常有着较大影响，隐含资产
质量评分由银行经营环境评分以及不良贷款率组成的矩阵映射得
出。基于隐含资产质量评分，惠誉博华也会参考关注类贷款占比、
逾期贷款占比、拨备覆盖率等指标进一步调整得出最终的资产质
量评分。此次测试结果显示，虽然部分头部城商行较国有银行及
股份制银行评分更佳，但整体上区域性银行资产质量评分结果落
后于全国展业的机构。测试样本中资产质量评分处于 bb 及以下类
别的银行主要为一些地处经济活力欠佳地区的中小型 区域银行 ，
整体测试样本中位评分为 bbb+。

收益和盈利能力  
持续较强的盈利能力可以为银行资本进行补充，提升其偿债能力。
收益和盈利能力隐含评分由银行经营环境评分以及 “营业利润/风
险加权资产 ”组成的矩阵映射得出。此外，惠誉博华还将考量银
行收益的稳定性、收入多元化程度、非利息收入占比、总资产收
益率和资本收益率等指标，对隐含评分进行调整并得出最终的评
分结果。本次测试结果中收益和盈利能力评分在 a 类别范围内的集
中度较高，占全部样本的约 38.1%。全部测试样本的收益和盈利能
力评分中位水平为 bbb+。

资本和杠杆水平 
充足的资本水平可以为银行经营过程中产生的损失提供吸收缓冲，
并维持其债务偿还能力，通常情况下资本和杠杆水平在财务状况
四项 KRD 中权重最高。资本和杠杆水平隐含评分由银行经营环境
评分以及 “核心一级资本充足率 ”组成的矩阵映射得出。在此基
础上，惠誉博华也会通过分析资本充足率、资本内生增长持续性、
高于监管最低要求的额外缓冲等方面进行 进一步 调整。国有银行
在资本方面同样表现出明显优于其他类型银行的评分，股份制银
行则分化明显。通常盈利能力较强的银行资本和杠杆水平评分也
会更好，反映了这部分机构更容易开展资本工具发行以及更好的

可持续内生资本补充能力。整体看测试样本的资本和杠杆水平中
位评分为 a-。

融资和流动性 
融资和流动性评分主要反映了银行对于其短期债务的履行能力。
惠誉博华的隐含融资和流动性评分由银行经营环境评分以及 “贷
款/客户存款”组成的矩阵映射得出。此外惠誉博华还将评估银行
的流动性覆盖情况、非存款融资占比、存款结构、对于经营外汇
业务占比高的银行还将评估外币的流动性等因素，在隐含评分基
础上进行调整。一般情况下融资和流动性是所有赋权 KRD 中权重
最低的，该项评分对隐含生存力评级的影响最小。 测试结果显示
中资商业银行流动性水平整体较好，评分中位级别为 a。

商业银行

附录 
评级框架 
惠誉博华对于特定银行整体信用质量的观点反映在最终评定的长期主体评级(LT IDR) 和/或短期主体评级(ST IDR) 上。长期主体评级用于评
估 13 个月(含)以上的信用状况，短期主体评级评估 13 个月(不含)以下信用水平。 
惠誉博华评定银行的长期主体评级兼顾了个体信用状况和潜在外部支持。银行的生存力评级(VR)反映了银行自身的信用水平；政府支持评
级(GSR)或股东支持评级(SSR)反映了特定银行在需要时获得外部支持的力度和可能性。短期主体评级根据与长期主体评级的对应表来确定
(详情请见“惠誉博华银行评级标准”)。 
根据下图所示，评定银行长期主体评级时，惠誉博华采用 “孰高原则 ”。银行的长期主体评级可以完全由其生存力评级驱动 (VR 高于
GSR/SSR)，也可以由政府支持评级或股东支持评级驱动(GSR/SSR 高于 VR)，或者由生存力评级和政府/股东支持评级共同驱动(GSR/SSR
与 VR 级别相同)。

来源：惠誉博华

### Page 7

研究报告│  20 October 2026 fitchbohua.cn 7

金融机构 
商业银行

惠誉博华通常根据最有可能的支持来源，评定银行的政府支持评级(GSR)或股东支持评级(SSR)。政府或股东支持评级反映了惠誉博华对特
定银行在需要时获得外部支持的力度和可能性的看法，外部支持通常来自政府机构 (含监管机构)或其股东。为对不同层级评级结果作出区
分，长期主体评级的评级结果为大写字母(AAA 序列)，而生存力评级、政府支持评级和股东支持评级的评级结果为小写字母(aaa 序列)。 
对于银行发行的债务工具评级，惠誉博华会根据相关债务工具的属性及偿付顺序在银行长期主体评级或生存力评级基础上进行调整。通
常情况下银行发行的高级无担保债券信用等级等同于其长期主体评级；次级或混合工具信用等级一般以其生存力评级为基准进行下调，
但满足少数特定条件的银行所发行的次级或混合资本工具也可能基于其长期主体评级进行调整。

我们对银行发行的最常见的长期债项进行评级的基准方法如下： 
• 高级无担保：等同于长期主体评级； 
• 次级债券（二级资本）：低于评级基点 0-1 个子级； 
• 混合（其他一级资本）：低于评级基点 0-2 个子级。 
次级债务的评级基点通常是银行的 VR。这反映了惠誉博华的观点，即额外的支持（不反映在 VR 中）一般不能延伸到非高级债务。2,3然
而，当惠誉博华认为股东支持有可能延伸到资本结构的更底层时，这些债务的评级基点可以用银行的长期主体评级。 4此外，如果受评银
行的系统重要性极强，与政府的关系十分密切，这种情况也可能导致政府支持覆盖到次级债务，次级债务的评级基点也可能是其长期主
体评级。下表简要介绍了债项和债务评级的基本原理： 
确定银行长期债项的评级基准 
 从基点进行评级调整 调整的基本原理 
债项类型 评级基点 不偿付 损失严重性 合计 不偿付 损失严重性 
高级无担保 长期主体评级 0 0 0 高级债务违约相当于银行破产。 预计违约时回收率处于平均水平。 
高级非优先 长期主体评级 0 0 0 
在银行生存力消失
前不存在“不偿付
风险”。 
预计违约时回收率
略低于平均水平。 
次级（二级） VR 或长期主体评级 0 0 或-1 0 或-1 在银行生存力消失前不存在“不偿付风险”。 预计违约时回收率较低。 
混合（一级） VR 或长期主体评级 0 或-1 0 或-1 0 至-2 在银行生存力消失前可能存在“不偿付风险”。 预计违约时回收率较低。 
来源：惠誉博华 
注：对于商业银行发行的科技创新债券等创新型债务融资工具，惠誉博华将根据其债券条款认定债项类型，并应用相应的评级调整。

2 如果一家银行没有被授予 VR，那么股东的 VR 或 LT IDR 可能是最合适的次级债的评级基点，或者惠誉博华可能会对不偿付和损失严重性风险进行更多的
定制分析（例如，在非经营性、停业银行的情况下）。 
3 如果惠誉博华认为一家银行的次级债券或混合工具在对第三方造成损失之前，很有可能将已经放在股东、其他相关方或政府实体那里的次级债务保释/转
为股权，那么在确定这些债券的评级基点和不偿付风险水平时，可能会从VR 进行上调。 
4 如果惠誉博华判断支持次级债务的可能性与支持高级债务的可能性一样，那么评级基点为长期主体评级。如果惠誉博华认为，相对于高级债务，次级债
务或混合债务的支持略低，那么我们评估的不偿付风险就会比 LT IDR 低一个子级。如果我们认为支持的概率更低，那么在 LT IDR 的基础上下调更多子级。

2026 年 10 月 1 日

経済レポート

日銀短観（2026 年 9 月調査）結果

調査部 主席研究員 小林真一郎 
研究員 小林啓介 
1. 大企業の業況判断 DI～製造業は改善、非製造業は悪化 
本日発表された日銀短観（2026 年 9 月調査）における大企業製造業の業況判断 DI（最近）は、前回調査から
2 ポイント改善の 24 となった。素材業種では、米国とイランの対立長期化に伴う原油価格の高止まりなどが引き
続き下押し要因 だが、原油の代替調達の進展 、販売価格へのコスト転嫁の浸透などを背景に 、石油・石炭製品
や化学などで 7 ポイント改善した。加工業種も、半導体関連をはじめ内外需要が堅調な生産用機械や電気機械
を中心に 2 ポイント改善した。加えて、為替相場が想定より円安に振れたことも下支えとなったとみられる。 
一方、大企業非製造業の業況判断 DI（最近）は、前回調査から 2 ポイント悪化の 35 となった。原油価格や人
件費の高止まりが、小売、宿泊・飲食サービス や対事業所サービスなどの幅広い業種の収益を圧迫したことに加
え、設備価格や金利の上昇が物品賃貸などを下押した。 
先行きについて、業況判断 DI（先行き）は、大企業製造業では 3 ポイント悪化の 21、大企業非製造業では 5
ポイント悪化の 30 と、いずれも悪化見込みとなった。製造業のうち素材業種では紙・パルプや化学、非鉄金属、
加工業種では金属製品、非製造業では物品賃貸や不動産の悪化幅が大きい。 米国とイランの対立は長期化し
ているものの、原材料の供給制約に対する懸念は一時期と比べて後退しているとみられる。しかし、原油価格の
高止まりや供給制約が再燃するリスクに加え、海外経済の先行きを巡る不透明感が引き続き意識され、慎重な見
通しになったと考えられる。

なお、2026 年度の想定為替レートは 154.23 円／ドルと、最近の為替相場の実勢よりも円高水準に設定され
た。足元の為替相場は 1 ドル＝158 円程度と、短観の想定為替レートを上回る円安水準となっている。先行きの
不透明感は強いが、このまま想定を上回る円安が続けば、自動車などの輸出企業の業績が上振れる一方、輸入
企業の多い非製造業や中小企業にとっては業績が下振れるリスクが生じる。

### Page 3

2. 中小企業の業況判断 DI～製造業は改善、非製造業は横ばい 
中小企業の業況判断 DI（最近）は、製造業では前回調査から 5 ポイント改善の 14、非製造業は横ばいの 15
となった。製造業では、価格転嫁の進展から、はん用機械や生産用機械、自動車などの加工業種を中心に改善
した。他方、非製造業では、宿泊・飲食サービスや物品賃貸などが悪化した一方、卸売や対個人サービスなどが
改善したことで、横ばいで推移した。ただし、製造業、非製造業ともに水準は高く、総じて見れば中小企業の業況
感も、大企業と同様に底堅い。 
他方で先行きについては、製造業では 2 ポイント悪化の 12、非製造業では 5 ポイント悪化の 10 が見込まれて
いる。製造業、非製造業ともに、イラン情勢悪化の長期化リスクが意識されるとともに、人手不足の深刻化 や金利
上昇などへの警戒感もあり、慎重な見通しとなっている。

図表 3 中小企業の業況判断 DI

### Page 4

3. 設備投資計画～2026 年度の設備投資計画は大企業を中心に堅調 
2026 年度の設備投資計画（含む土地投資、除くソフトウェア・研究開発投資額）は、大企業製造業では前年比
＋11.6%と上方修正される一方、非製造業では同＋11.2%と下方修正されたが、いずれも前年比 2 桁のプラスの
計画が維持されている。企業の設備投資意欲は、設備投資コストや金利の上昇が重しとなる中でも、堅調な企業
業績や、イラン情勢悪化に伴う供給制約懸念の後退を背景に、良好な状態を維持している。こうした中、老朽設
備の維持・更新投資のほか、人手不足に対応するための省力化・効率化投資、E コマースの拡大を背景とした先
進物流施設などの建設投資、脱炭素に向けた環境対応投資を中心に、堅調な増加が維持されよう。 
また中小企業では、製造業では前年比＋4.0%、非製造業では同－8.4%と、いずれも前回調査から上方修正
された。非製造業は前年比マイナスの計画となっているが、大企業と同様に中小企業においても設備投資意欲
は良好であることから、今後、投資計画の進捗に伴って例年通りに上方修正されていくと見込まれる。 
なお、原油やナフサの代替調達の進展により、ホルムズ海峡封鎖に伴う数量面での供給制約懸念は一時期と
比べて後退しているものの、再度の情勢悪化により供給制約が再燃するリスクは残る。加えて、日中関係の悪化
継続によるグローバルサプライチェーンの混乱 や人手不足の深刻化、種々の設備導入コストの増加、日本銀行
の利上げなど、企業の設備投資意欲の減退や計画の見直し・先送りにつながるリスク要因は多い。このため、設
備投資の進捗が現時点の計画よりも下振れるリスクがある点には、引き続き注意を要する。

南俊國際 (6584 TT)  Buy

伺服器導軌展望樂觀，投資建議 Buy。  
投資建議 ：高階伺服器滑軌布局持續推進， Vera Rubin 和 ASIC
新專案有望帶動營收及獲利成長，因此給予 Buy 的投資建議，
目標價 579 元 (以 2027 年預估 EPS 及 25 倍本益比評價 )。  
3Q26 營收季減， 4Q26AI 專案量產是營收回升關鍵 ：3Q26 營收
8.31 億元， QoQ-1.8% ，主要反映通用型伺服器客戶受供應鏈缺
料影響而放緩拉貨。目前 Vera Rubin 已小量出貨，預估 4Q26 出
貨量逐月增長， A 客戶氣冷機櫃亦已於 10/2026 出貨，將有助營
收回升。  
Vera Rubin 與 ASIC 為 2027 主要成長動能： Vera Rubin 已於
08/2026 開始小量出貨，預估 4Q26 可逐月增長，並於 1Q27 放
量。 2027 年將有 M 客戶 ASIC 平台將投入量產。  
通用型伺服器需求強勁，但受供應端衝擊 ：目前通用型伺服器
營收佔比仍高於 AI 伺服器，然而受零組件缺料影響， 3Q26 出
貨較不順暢。南俊已取得 Vera CPU rack 及 M 客戶 Storage rack
訂單，預期 2027 年將推動營收顯著增長。  
越南成熟產線與台灣新品自動化並進，成本優勢改善毛利率 ：
公司持續提高首條 Vera Rubin 產線自動化。越南製造成本優勢
提供成本端支撐，高階產品則有助提升附加價值，兩者是毛利
率改善的主要營動能。  
預估 4Q26 營收、毛利率季增 ：群益預估 4Q26 營收 8.99 億元，
QoQ+8.18% ，毛利率 45.0% 、營益率 33.5% ，稅後淨利 2.47 億
元、 EPS 3.46 元。 2026 年全年營收 34.44 億元， YoY+41.55% ，
